<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the compact log-smooth Kähler case of b-semiampleness. Let $`f:Y\to X`$ be a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers between smooth compact connected Kähler manifolds, and let Δ be an effective rational divisor with simple normal crossing support and coefficients in $`[0,1]`$, with $`K_Y+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb Q}f^*L`$ for $`L\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}\nolimits (X)_{\mathbb Q}`$. There is a smooth compact Kähler modification $`S\to X`$ for which the threshold-moduli line satisfies $`M_{S_1}=\nu^*M_S`$ in $`\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}\nolimits (S_1)_{\mathbb Q}`$ for every smooth compact Kähler modification $`\nu:S_1\to S`$, and some positive multiple of <i>M</i><sub><i>S</i></sub> is represented by a holomorphic line bundle generated by global sections. Horizontal components of coefficient one are allowed; neither projectivity nor a Campana orbifold Iitaka hypothesis is assumed.

- **stmt-thm-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Under the assumptions above, $\mathbf M$ is b-semiample. More explicitly, there is a smooth compact Kähler modification $\mu_0:S\to X$ such that:* 1. *for every smooth compact Kähler modification $\nu:S_1\to S$, $$M_{S_1}=\nu^*M_S\quad\text{in }\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(S_1)_{\mathbb Q};$$* 2. *for some positive integer $m$, the actual line bundle representing $mM_S$ is generated by its global holomorphic sections.* *The theorem includes a point base and relative dimension zero. No projectivity of $f$, $X$, or $Y$ is assumed.
  - Explanation: The threshold-defined moduli rational b-line bundle stabilizes on a smooth compact Kähler model and has a globally generated positive multiple.
- **stmt-prelim-threshold** (bound, [source](#stmt-prelim-threshold)): Fix a model $f':(Y',\Delta')\to X'$ as in <a href="#eq:moduli" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:moduli">[eq:moduli]</a> and a prime $P\subset X'$. On a joint log resolution of $\Delta'$ and $(f')^*P$, let $\delta_E$ be the crepant coefficient and $a_E=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E((f')^*P)>0$. Over a general point of $P$, $$t_P=\min_{E\mapsto P}\frac{1-\delta_E}{a_E}.$$ In particular, the threshold is rational and unchanged by a further resolution of the source. The divisor $B_{X'}$ has finite support.
  - Explanation: A joint simple normal crossing resolution reduces the threshold to a finite minimum and shows finite discriminant support.
- **stmt-hodge-rootpoles** (bound, [source](#stmt-hodge-rootpoles)): Over $V^\circ$, the form $\tau$ is a relative top form with at most logarithmic poles on $H$ and no other poles. At a strict covering prime over a horizontal divisor of coefficient $\delta$, if $j$ is the ramification index, its order is $$j(1-\delta)-1.$$
  - Explanation: The relative root form has only the allowed logarithmic boundary poles, with its order determined by ramification and the boundary coefficient.
- **stmt-hodge-purerules** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hodge-purerules)): Let a real-polarizable variation of pure Hodge structure be given on the complement of an SNC divisor in a polydisk, with quasi-unipotent local monodromies.* 1. *After coordinate power substitutions making all local monodromies unipotent, its Hodge filtrations extend by subbundles of the canonical flat bundle. On a disk, the filtration also extends by subbundles of either the upper or the lower canonical bundle before unipotentization.* 2. *After coordinate power substitutions making the monodromies unipotent, the canonical extension and its Hodge filtration commute with further powers and with pullback to any disk whose punctured image lies in the given open.* 3. *A real pure Hodge morphism has flat Hodge kernels and images, with flat Hodge complements. Its split maps and finite-group eigenspace projectors are compatible with the canonical extensions in <span class="upright">(i)</span>. After unipotentization, tensor constructions are compatible as well.* 4. *On a unipotent disk, a nonvanishing frame $e$ of an extended highest Hodge line satisfies, for some constants $C,A>0$, $$C^{-1}(1+\|\log\|t\|\|)^{-A} \leq \\|e(t)\\| \leq C(1+\|\log\|t\|\|)^A. \label{hodge:logbounds}$$ The constants may depend on the disk. The same assertion applies to Hodge determinant lines.
  - Explanation: Pure Hodge extensions preserve filtrations, split morphisms and tensor constructions under the specified monodromy conditions, with two-sided logarithmic norm control.
- **stmt-hodge-doublegrades** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hodge-doublegrades)): Let a mixed variation on a punctured disk be extended in its upper canonical flat bundle, with the canonical weight filtration and locally free double Hodge/weight grades, as in <a href="#hodge:FF" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="hodge:FF">[hodge:FF]</a>. Suppose a finite-group character summand satisfies $$\dim F^d=1,\qquad F^{d+1}=0,$$ and the sole nonzero $F^d$ weight grade has weight $w$. Then projection to $\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}^W_w$ identifies the extended top line with the canonically extended top line of that pure grade.
  - Explanation: Local freeness of double grades identifies the extended mixed top line with the unique pure weight grade carrying it.
- **stmt-hodge-residue** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hodge-residue)): Let $k$ be the maximal number of horizontal coefficient-one components meeting in a good fiber, constant after shrinking $U$. For the local root family above:* 1. *$$F^d\mathbb V_\chi=\mathcal O_{V^\circ}\tau.$$ This line lies in the single pure weight grade $\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}^W_{d+k}\mathbb V_\chi$.* 2. *Choose a depth-$k$ intersection component downstairs dominating the good open. After a good-open base change selecting one connected component $A_x^*$ of its fibers, take every root-cover sheet above that component. Iterated residue identifies the line in <span class="upright">(i)</span>, through its pure grade and with the Tate twist removed, with the highest eigenline of the resolved root cover of the induced klt volume on $A_x^*$. In degree $m$ this is the specified plurivolume identification from SNC adjunction.* 3. *These highest-line identifications preserve generators in canonical extensions on unipotent disks. In a disk model satisfying the hypotheses of <a href="#hodge:FF" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="hodge:FF">[hodge:FF]</a>, its extended mixed top line projects isomorphically to the same pure line.* *If $k=0$, the intersection is the whole original fiber, there is no removed divisor, and the pure root-cover cohomology itself provides the line.
  - Explanation: Iterated residue identifies the root eigenline with the highest line of a deepest klt stratum and preserves canonical extended generators.
- **stmt-hodge-birational** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hodge-birational)): Over a smooth good open, let $\varphi:(Z',H')\to(Z,H)$ be a proper equivariant modification between smooth families of SNC pairs, with $H'$ the reduced inverse image of $H$. Pullback is an isomorphism on top logarithmic-form spaces. In the character summand of Proposition <a href="#hodge:residue" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hodge:residue">7</a>, it identifies the top line and the top line of its unique pure weight grade.* *Suppose further that, over a disk, the new compactification satisfies the hypotheses of <a href="#hodge:FF" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="hodge:FF">[hodge:FF]</a>. If the original variation has been made unipotent, testing extension of the pulled-back volume in $g'_*\omega_{Z'/\Delta}(H')$ tests the canonical extension of the original top line. It is not necessary to assume unipotence of every summand newly appearing in the cohomology of $Z'\setminus H'$.
  - Explanation: Equivariant modifications preserve top logarithmic-form spaces and the relevant pure top line, including the disk extension test.
- **stmt-orders-threshold** (bound, [source](#stmt-orders-threshold)): Fix a prime divisor $P\subset S$. On a resolution over a general point of $P$, arrange joint SNC support for the crepant divisor and $p^*P$. Write $\delta_E$ for the crepant coefficient and $a_E=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(p^*P)>0$ for the components dominating $P$. Then $$t_P=\min_{E\mapsto P}\frac{1-\delta_E}{a_E}. \label{orders:thresholdformula}$$ This minimum computes the threshold over all divisorial valuations, including valuations exceptional over the chosen resolution.
  - Explanation: The finite threshold minimum also tests exceptional divisorial valuations beyond the chosen resolution.
- **stmt-orders-diskmodels** (construction, [source](#stmt-orders-diskmodels)): Fix the local root family of Section <a href="#hodge:rootconstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hodge:rootconstruction">3.1</a> and a resolved transverse disk downstairs. For every sufficiently divisible power substitution $t=u^N$, there is a smooth equivariant Kähler model over $\Delta_u$ with the following properties:* 1. *It retains every component of the generic root cover and maps to the downstairs order resolution. On the punctured disk it is a modification of the pulled-back root family.* 2. *The horizontal removed divisor $H_N$, defined by the reduced full inverse image of the original removed divisor on the punctured disk and then closed up, is SNC. Its support and the full central fiber have joint SNC support.* 3. *Every stratum of $(Z_N,H_N)$ dominates the disk and is smooth over a smaller punctured disk.* 4. *For each vertical prime $E$ on the downstairs order resolution, some prime on $Z_N$ dominates $E$.* *Consequently <a href="#hodge:FF" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="hodge:FF">[hodge:FF]</a> applies to this model, and its top eigenline tests the extension of the original line as in Lemma <a href="#hodge:birational" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hodge:birational">8</a>.
  - Explanation: Resolved power-substitution models retain all generic root sheets and the primes needed for extension tests.
- **stmt-orders-order** (bound, [source](#stmt-orders-order)): The section $\tau$ is meromorphic in that extended line. Its normalized order is $$\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}^{\mathrm{Hdg}}_0(\tau) =\min_E\frac{l_E+1-a_E}{a_E}, \label{orders:generalorder}$$ where $E$ runs through the central-fiber primes on the downstairs resolution. The normalization divides an ordinary order after $t=u^N$ by $N$. At the displayed order, the corrected section generates the extension after clearing its denominator.
  - Explanation: The root volume is meromorphic in the canonical extended line, and a finite valuation minimum computes its normalized order.
- **stmt-orders-trace** (theorem, [source](#stmt-orders-trace)): In the setup of <a href="#orders:adjoint" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="orders:adjoint">[orders:adjoint]</a>, choose locally a frame $s$ of $\mathcal O_S(mQ_S)$, and let $\tau$ be its tautological root as in Section <a href="#hodge:rootconstruction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hodge:rootconstruction">3.1</a>. For every prime $P\subset S$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_P^{\mathrm{Hdg}}(\tau)=t_P-1. \label{orders:traceorder}$$* *More precisely, let $\pi:\widetilde V\to V$ be a coordinate power chart making the local pure variation unipotent, and let $\overline{\mathcal E}_\chi$ be its extended highest eigenline. For a sufficiently divisible positive integer $a$, the open identification $s^{\otimes a}\leftrightarrow \tau^{\otimes am}$ extends to an isomorphism of actual bundles $$\pi^*\mathcal O_S(amM_S)\|_{\widetilde V} \simeq \overline{\mathcal E}_\chi^{\otimes am}. \label{orders:tracebundle}$$ One integer $a$ suffices on the compact base. The resulting extension rule commutes with pullback to disks generically meeting $U$, after further power substitution on the disk. All assertions allow arbitrary rational coefficients on the vertical components of $D_W$.
  - Explanation: The canonical volume extension equals the threshold trace as an actual rational holomorphic line, with the specified open identification and disk pullback rule.
- **stmt-orders-bdescent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-orders-bdescent)): For the original fibration and the smooth compact Kähler model $S$ fixed above, every smooth compact Kähler modification $\nu:S_1\to S$ satisfies $$M_{S_1}=\nu^*M_S \quad\text{in }\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(S_1)\otimes\mathbb Q.$$ No assumption that $\nu$ be an isomorphism over $U$ is needed.
  - Explanation: The actual rational moduli line pulls back under every further smooth compact Kähler modification, including modifications over the good open.
- **stmt-orders-residuenorm** (theorem, [source](#stmt-orders-residuenorm)): The expression <a href="#orders:residuenormformula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="orders:residuenormformula">[orders:residuenormformula]</a> is a positive finite norm, homogeneous of degree one, on the volume line. Let $a$ clear the indicated powers. Along any disk in $P$ whose punctured part lies in $P^\circ$, after a sufficiently divisible power substitution, a nonvanishing local frame of $$r^*\mathcal O_S(amM_S)$$ and its dual have at most powers-of-log growth in the $a$-th tensor power of this norm and its dual norm. No Kähler assumption on $P$ is required.
  - Explanation: The residue integral gives a positive finite homogeneous volume norm and two-sided logarithmic control on extended generators and their duals.
- **stmt-spread-marked** (construction, [source](#stmt-spread-marked)): There are a smooth compact Kähler manifold $\Sigma$, a proper surjection $\rho:\Sigma\to S$, and a dense open $\Sigma^\circ\subseteq\rho^{-1}(U)$ with the following data:* 1. *a proper smooth family $a:\mathscr A^\circ\to\Sigma^\circ$ with connected fibers and a holomorphic section;* 2. *an effective relative SNC rational boundary $D_{\mathscr A}$ with coefficients in $[0,1)$;* 3. *an identification $$a_*\mathcal O_{\mathscr A^\circ} \bigl(m(K_{\mathscr A^\circ/\Sigma^\circ}+D_{\mathscr A})\bigr) \simeq \rho^*\mathcal G_m.$$ The relative log-pluri bundle itself is the pullback of this line. Moreover, $\mathscr A^\circ$ and its boundary have extensions in a smooth compact Kähler manifold mapping to $\Sigma$.* *Each fiber pair is a connected component of a deepest coefficient-one stratum of the original good family, with its adjunction boundary.
  - Explanation: Deepest coefficient-one strata produce a marked connected klt family with a section and the specified adjunction volume line.
- **stmt-spread-residue-growth** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spread-residue-growth)): Let $P$ be a smooth compact complex manifold and $r:P\to S$ a holomorphic map factoring through the marked-family construction on a dense open $P^\circ$. Equip $r^*\mathcal G_m$ there with <a href="#spread:residue-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spread:residue-norm">[spread:residue-norm]</a>. Along a disk in $P$ mapping generically to $P^\circ$, generators of any sufficiently divisible positive power of $r^*(mM_S)$, and the dual generators, have at most powers-of-logarithm growth in the corresponding norms, after a sufficiently divisible power substitution.
  - Explanation: The marked residue norm controls sufficiently divisible pulled-back moduli powers and their duals along generically good disks.
- **stmt-spread-fiber-splitting** (construction, [source](#stmt-spread-fiber-splitting)): Let $(F,D)$ be a smooth connected compact Kähler pair, with effective SNC rational boundary of coefficients less than one, and suppose that $m(K_F+D)$ is trivial. There is a finite étale cover $\nu:J\to F$ and an isomorphism of pairs $$\label{spread:fiber-product} (J,\nu^*D)\simeq (Q,C)\times T_1\times\cdots\times T_j,$$ where $(Q,C)$ is a smooth projective klt SNC pair, and each $T_i$ is a positive-dimensional complex torus or an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold. The boundary is entirely on $Q$. A point is allowed for $Q$, and $j=0$ is allowed. Moreover, $m(K_Q+C)$ and all $mK_{T_i}$ are trivial.
  - Explanation: A finite étale cover splits a klt log-pluricanonically trivial Kähler fiber into a projective boundary-bearing factor and boundary-free torus or symplectic factors.
- **stmt-spread-finite-covers** (construction, [source](#stmt-spread-finite-covers)): Let $g:E^\circ\to B^\circ$ be a proper smooth holomorphic map of connected complex manifolds, with connected compact fibers and a section. Suppose $B^\circ$ is a dense open in a smooth compact Kähler manifold $B$, and the family has a smooth compact Kähler extension $E\to B$. Then all finite connected étale covers of individual fibers occur in countably many families, each obtained after a finite étale base cover of $B^\circ$. Each resulting base and total space admit smooth compact Kähler extensions preserving the open families. The same holds with pulled-back relative SNC boundary data.
  - Explanation: Finite fiber covers spread in countably many families after finite étale base covers, preserving compact Kähler extensions and boundary data.
- **stmt-spread-constructible** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spread-constructible)): Let $q:Z\to T$ be a proper holomorphic map of compact complex spaces. The image of a constructible subset of $Z$ is constructible in $T$. Consequently, any finite combination of incidence, emptiness, surjectivity, and injectivity conditions on fibers of compact analytic diagrams is constructible.
  - Explanation: Proper holomorphic images preserve constructibility in compact complex spaces and allow finite combinations of fiberwise incidence conditions.
- **stmt-spread-product** (construction, [source](#stmt-spread-product)): After a proper surjection $r:P\to S$, with $P$ a smooth compact connected manifold, there is a dense open $P^\circ\subseteq r^{-1}(U)$ on which the marked klt family has a finite étale cover and a product decomposition $$\label{spread:family-product} (\mathscr J,D_{\mathscr J}) \simeq (\mathscr Q,C)\times_{P^\circ} \mathscr T_1\times_{P^\circ}\cdots\times_{P^\circ}\mathscr T_j .$$ Here all families are proper and smooth with connected fibers. They have the following properties.* 1. *The pair $(\mathscr Q,C)$ is a projective family of effective klt SNC pairs. It is embedded in $P^\circ\times\mathbb P^N$ and is the pullback of universal marked families by the restriction of a holomorphic map from $P$ to a fixed product of projective Hilbert schemes.* 2. *Each $\mathscr T_i$ is a family of submanifolds of a fixed compact Kähler manifold $V$, of positive dimension $n_i$. Its cohomological local systems have their integral lattices modulo torsion and flat real polarizations. Its top Hodge line has rank one and is given by $$\lambda_i =(\pi_i)_*\omega_{\mathscr T_i/P^\circ} =F^{n_i}R^{n_i}(\pi_i)_*\mathbb C$$ Each index is of one of the following two types: $$\begin{array}{ll} \text{torus type:}& h^{1,0}(T_i)=n_i,\quad \bigwedge^{n_i}F^1R^1(\pi_i)_*\mathbb C \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\lambda_i;\\[2mm] \text{symplectic type:}& n_i\ \text{even},\quad h^{2,0}(T_i)=1,\quad \bigl(F^2R^2(\pi_i)_*\mathbb C\bigr)^{\otimes n_i/2} \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\lambda_i . \end{array}$$ The displayed maps are cup products on the underlying variations.* 3. *On every fiber, $m(K_Q+C)$ and $mK_{T_i}$ are trivial. With $$\mathcal G_{m,Q} =(\pi_Q)_*\mathcal O_{\mathscr Q} \bigl(m(K_{\mathscr Q/P^\circ}+C)\bigr),$$ there is an identification of actual holomorphic line bundles $$\label{spread:volume-line} r^*\mathcal G_m \simeq \mathcal G_{m,Q}\otimes \bigotimes_i\lambda_i^{\otimes m}.$$* 4. *If the finite cover in <a href="#spread:family-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spread:family-product">[spread:family-product]</a> has degree $e$, the norm in <a href="#spread:residue-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spread:residue-norm">[spread:residue-norm]</a> satisfies $$\label{spread:norm-product} \\|s\\|_{\mathrm{res}} =c\,\\|s_Q\\|_{Q,\mathrm{res}}\, \prod_i\\|\omega_i\\|_{\mathrm{Hdg}}^m \quad\text{when}\quad s\longleftrightarrow s_Q\otimes\bigotimes_i\omega_i^{\otimes m}.$$ Here $c>0$ is constant; with compatible volume conventions, $c=e^{-m/2}$. The norm on the left gives generators and their duals of sufficiently divisible powers of $r^*(mM_S)$ the two-sided logarithmic bounds of Lemma <a href="#spread:residue-growth" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spread:residue-growth">16</a>.* *These assertions are preserved on the good open under further smooth compact auxiliary base changes. Neither generic finiteness of $r$ nor a global Kähler form on $P$ is required.
  - Explanation: A compact proper auxiliary surjection carries an actual product decomposition on a dense open, with compatible cohomological types, volume lines and norms.
- **stmt-compare-semiample** (theorem, [source](#stmt-compare-semiample)): Use the notation of Proposition <a href="#spread:product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spread:product">20</a>, and write $P^\circ\subset P$ for its good open set. After a proper modification $\pi:P'\to P$, with $P'$ smooth compact, and a further shrinking of $P^\circ$, there are a positive integer $a$, a globally generated holomorphic line bundle $\mathcal H_Q$ on $P'$, and a specified isomorphism $$\label{compare:open-identification} \mathcal H_Q\|_{\pi^{-1}(P^\circ)} \simeq \bigl(\pi^*\mathcal G_{m,Q}\bigr)^{\otimes a} \|_{\pi^{-1}(P^\circ)} .$$ Transport to the left the $a$-th power of the volume norm $$N_{m,Q}(s)= \left(\int_{Q_x}\|s\|^{2/m}\right)^{m/2}.$$ For any holomorphic disk in $P'$ whose punctured disk lies in the good open, a local generator of the pulled-back $\mathcal H_Q$ and its dual have norm bounded by powers of $1+\|\log\|t\|\|$, after a finite power substitution if necessary.
  - Explanation: The projective factor volume line has a globally generated extension after modification, preserving its specified open identification and logarithmic norms.
- **stmt-periods-rationalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-periods-rationalization)): Let $(\mathbb V_{\mathbb Z},F^\bullet)$ be an integral pure variation with a flat real polarization $B$. There is a flat real automorphism $C$ of $\mathbb V_{\mathbb R}$ such that $(\mathbb V_{\mathbb Z},C^{-1}F^\bullet)$ is polarized by a rational form $B'$, and $$B'(x,y)=B(Cx,Cy).$$ Consequently the new and original variations are isomorphic as real polarized variations, and their holomorphic Hodge bundles are isomorphic. Their canonical Hodge extensions are also identified wherever local monodromy is unipotent. After multiplying $B'$ by a positive integer, its values on $\mathbb V_{\mathbb Z}$ are integral.
  - Explanation: A flat real automorphism transports the filtration to a rationally polarized variation on the same integral local system, preserving the holomorphic bundles and unipotent extensions.
- **stmt-periods-lefschetz** (theorem, [source](#stmt-periods-lefschetz)): Let $t:\mathcal T\to B^\circ$ be a smooth proper family of connected compact complex manifolds of dimension $n$ over a complex manifold $B^\circ$, and suppose a closed real $(1,1)$-form on $\mathcal T$ restricts to a Kähler form on every fiber. Its restriction class $\kappa$ is flat. The cohomological variations, with their integral lattices modulo torsion, are real-polarizable. In particular:* 1. *on $H^1$, a polarization is $$B_1(\alpha,\beta)=\int_{\mathcal T_b} \alpha\wedge\beta\wedge\kappa^{n-1};$$* 2. *if $n\geq2$, write $H^2_{\mathbb R}=P^2_{\mathbb R}\oplus\mathbb R\kappa$, where $P^2_{\mathbb R}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}(\kappa^{n-1}\cup-)$. For $\alpha=\alpha_0+a\kappa$ and $\beta=\beta_0+b\kappa$, a full weight-two polarization is $$\label{periods:full-h2} B_2(\alpha,\beta)= \int_{\mathcal T_b}\alpha_0\wedge\beta_0\wedge\kappa^{n-2} -ab\int_{\mathcal T_b}\kappa^n.$$ If $h^{2,0}=1$, this real form has signature $(2,h^{1,1})$.
  - Explanation: A closed fiberwise Kähler form supplies flat real polarizations, including the full degree-two polarization with its required sign on the Kähler line.
- **stmt-periods-extensions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-periods-extensions)): Let a pure real-polarizable integral variation be defined on the complement of an SNC divisor in a complex manifold, with unipotent local monodromy. Canonical Hodge extensions commute with tensor products, exterior powers, duals, and holomorphic pullbacks from smooth manifolds with SNC boundary, whose boundary complements map into the original complement. This includes disks whose punctured disks map into that complement. A morphism of pure real variations induces an isomorphism between its coimage and image on these extensions. In particular, an isomorphism of highest Hodge lines induced by such a morphism remains an isomorphism after extension.* *If $\mathcal H$ is a highest Hodge line, $\gamma:\Delta\to B$ is a disk with $\gamma(\Delta^*)$ in the open set, and $e$ is a nowhere-zero local section of $\gamma^*\overline{\mathcal H}$, there are constants $c>0$ and $a\geq0$ such that, for $0<\|z\|$ sufficiently small, $$\label{periods:logbounds} c^{-1}(1+\|\log\|z\|\|)^{-a} \ \leq\ \\|e(z)\\|\ \leq\ c(1+\|\log\|z\|\|)^a.$$ The same assertion holds for its powers and their duals.
  - Explanation: Unipotent canonical Hodge extensions preserve tensor operations, pullbacks and pure morphism images, with two-sided highest-line norm estimates.
- **stmt-periods-griffiths** (theorem, [source](#stmt-periods-griffiths)): Let $B$ be a smooth compact complex manifold, let $D$ be an SNC divisor, and let $\mathbb V$ be a real-polarizable integral variation on $B^\circ=B\setminus D$. Suppose its Hodge numbers are either $$w=1,\quad h^{1,0}=h^{0,1}=g, \qquad\text{or}\qquad w=2,\quad h^{2,0}=h^{0,2}=1.$$ After a finite cover of $B^\circ$, extended and resolved over $B$, the canonical extension of its Griffiths line is semiample. The resulting base is smooth and compact and its boundary can be taken SNC.
  - Explanation: Classical period domains give semiample Griffiths-line extensions on compact analytic bases after finite open covers and resolution.
- **stmt-periods-semiample** (theorem, [source](#stmt-periods-semiample)): Let $r:P\to S$ and the smooth families $t_i:T_i\to P^\circ$ be as in Proposition <a href="#spread:product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spread:product">20</a>, with $n_i=\dim T_{i,u}>0$, and put $$\mathcal H_i=F^{n_i}\bigl(R^{n_i}(t_i)_*\mathbb C\otimes\mathcal O_{P^\circ}\bigr).$$ Thus $\mathcal H_i$ is the highest line denoted $\lambda_i$ in Proposition <a href="#spread:product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spread:product">20</a>; the symbol $\lambda$ above denotes the Griffiths line. After a proper generically finite surjection $\pi:P'\to P$, obtained by extending a finite *étale* cover of the good open and resolving, there are semiample holomorphic line bundles $\overline{\mathcal H}_i$ on the smooth compact manifold $P'$ whose specified restrictions are $\pi^*\mathcal H_i$.* *The boundary may be taken SNC. Along any disk in $P'$ whose punctured disk lies in the good open, a generator of $\overline{\mathcal H}_i$ and its inverse have at most powers-of-logarithm growth in the volume norm <a href="#periods:volume" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="periods:volume">[periods:volume]</a> and its dual. The same is true for all positive powers, including the powers used in the product-volume identification of Proposition <a href="#spread:product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spread:product">20</a>.
  - Explanation: A common compact cover supplies semiample highest-line extensions for the period factors with their geometric volume norms.
- **stmt-descent-unit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-descent-unit)): Let $P$ be a complex manifold, $D$ a simple normal crossing divisor, and $P^\circ=P\setminus D$. Let $E_1,E_2$ be holomorphic line bundles on $P$, equipped on $P^\circ$ with Hermitian norms, and let $$\phi:E_1\|_{P^\circ}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }E_2\|_{P^\circ}$$ be a specified holomorphic isomorphism. Assume the following near the smooth locus of each component of $D$. In a coordinate polydisk $(t,z)$ with $D=(t=0)$, choose nonvanishing holomorphic frames $e_1,e_2$. For a dense set of centers $z$, their norms and dual norms along the parallel punctured disks satisfy bounds by powers of $1+\|\log\|t\|\|$. The constants and powers may depend on the disk. Assume also that $\phi$ compares the two norms by factors bounded above and below on each such disk. It is enough for these bounds to hold after a finite power substitution on each disk. Then $\phi$ extends uniquely to a holomorphic isomorphism $E_1\simeq E_2$ on $P$.
  - Explanation: Two-sided logarithmic bounds extend a specified open line-bundle isomorphism and its inverse uniquely across the boundary.
- **stmt-descent-semiample** (theorem, [source](#stmt-descent-semiample)): Let $r:P\to S$ be a proper surjective holomorphic map of connected compact complex manifolds, and let $A$ be a holomorphic line bundle on $S$. If $r^*A$ is semiample, then $A$ is semiample. More precisely, let $$P\xrightarrow{h}T\xrightarrow{q}S$$ be the Stein factorization, and let $d$ be the generic degree of $q$. If $r^*(A^{\otimes n})$ is globally generated, then $A^{\otimes nd}$ is globally generated. The same descent conclusion holds for rational holomorphic line bundles after clearing denominators.
  - Explanation: Semiampleness descends through a proper surjection using Stein factorization and finite analytic norms, with a common global-generation exponent.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hodge-purerules",
        "exact_quote": "Let a real-polarizable variation of pure Hodge structure be given on the complement of an SNC divisor in a polydisk, with quasi-unipotent local monodromies.*\n\n1.  *After coordinate power substitutions making all local monodromies unipotent, its Hodge filtrations extend by subbundles of the canonical flat bundle. On a disk, the filtration also extends by subbundles of either the upper or the lower canonical bundle before unipotentization.*\n\n2.  *After coordinate power substitutions making the monodromies unipotent, the canonical extension and its Hodge filtration commute with further powers and with pullback to any disk whose punctured image lies in the given open.*\n\n3.  *A real pure Hodge morphism has flat Hodge kernels and images, with flat Hodge complements. Its split maps and finite-group eigenspace projectors are compatible with the canonical extensions in <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span>. After unipotentization, tensor constructions are compatible as well.*\n\n\n4.  *On a unipotent disk, a nonvanishing frame $e$ of an extended highest Hodge line satisfies, for some constants $C,A>0$, $$C^{-1}(1+|\\log|t||)^{-A}\n     \\leq \\|e(t)\\|\n     \\leq C(1+|\\log|t||)^A.\n     \\label{hodge:logbounds}$$ The constants may depend on the disk. The same assertion applies to Hodge determinant lines.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hodge-doublegrades",
        "exact_quote": "Let a mixed variation on a punctured disk be extended in its upper canonical flat bundle, with the canonical weight filtration and locally free double Hodge/weight grades, as in <a href=\"#hodge:FF\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"hodge:FF\">[hodge:FF]</a>. Suppose a finite-group character summand satisfies $$\\dim F^d=1,\\qquad F^{d+1}=0,$$ and the sole nonzero $F^d$ weight grade has weight $w$. Then projection to $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}^W_w$ identifies the extended top line with the canonically extended top line of that pure grade.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.5"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hodge-residue",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be the maximal number of horizontal coefficient-one components meeting in a good fiber, constant after shrinking $U$. For the local root family above:*\n\n1.  *$$F^d\\mathbb V_\\chi=\\mathcal O_{V^\\circ}\\tau.$$ This line lies in the single pure weight grade $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Gr}}^W_{d+k}\\mathbb V_\\chi$.*\n\n2.  *Choose a depth-$k$ intersection component downstairs dominating the good open. After a good-open base change selecting one connected component $A_x^*$ of its fibers, take every root-cover sheet above that component. Iterated residue identifies the line in <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span>, through its pure grade and with the Tate twist removed, with the highest eigenline of the resolved root cover of the induced klt volume on $A_x^*$. In degree $m$ this is the specified plurivolume identification from SNC adjunction.*\n\n3.  *These highest-line identifications preserve generators in canonical extensions on unipotent disks. In a disk model satisfying the hypotheses of <a href=\"#hodge:FF\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"hodge:FF\">[hodge:FF]</a>, its extended mixed top line projects isomorphically to the same pure line.*\n\n*If $k=0$, the intersection is the whole original fiber, there is no removed divisor, and the pure root-cover cohomology itself provides the line.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-hodge-birational",
        "exact_quote": "Over a smooth good open, let $\\varphi:(Z',H')\\to(Z,H)$ be a proper equivariant modification between smooth families of SNC pairs, with $H'$ the reduced inverse image of $H$. Pullback is an isomorphism on top logarithmic-form spaces. In the character summand of Proposition <a href=\"#hodge:residue\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"hodge:residue\">7</a>, it identifies the top line and the top line of its unique pure weight grade.*\n\n*Suppose further that, over a disk, the new compactification satisfies the hypotheses of <a href=\"#hodge:FF\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"hodge:FF\">[hodge:FF]</a>. If the original variation has been made unipotent, testing extension of the pulled-back volume in $g'_*\\omega_{Z'/\\Delta}(H')$ tests the canonical extension of the original top line. It is not necessary to assume unipotence of every summand newly appearing in the cohomology of $Z'\\setminus H'$.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.5"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-orders-threshold",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a prime divisor $P\\subset S$. On a resolution over a general point of $P$, arrange joint SNC support for the crepant divisor and $p^*P$. Write $\\delta_E$ for the crepant coefficient and $a_E=\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_E(p^*P)>0$ for the components dominating $P$. Then $$t_P=\\min_{E\\mapsto P}\\frac{1-\\delta_E}{a_E}.\n \\label{orders:thresholdformula}$$ This minimum computes the threshold over all divisorial valuations, including valuations exceptional over the chosen resolution.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-orders-threshold",

          "source_id": "PM-033.5"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-orders-diskmodels",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the local root family of Section <a href=\"#hodge:rootconstruction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"hodge:rootconstruction\">3.1</a> and a resolved transverse disk downstairs. For every sufficiently divisible power substitution $t=u^N$, there is a smooth equivariant Kähler model over $\\Delta_u$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *It retains every component of the generic root cover and maps to the downstairs order resolution. On the punctured disk it is a modification of the pulled-back root family.*\n\n2.  *The horizontal removed divisor $H_N$, defined by the reduced full inverse image of the original removed divisor on the punctured disk and then closed up, is SNC. Its support and the full central fiber have joint SNC support.*\n\n3.  *Every stratum of $(Z_N,H_N)$ dominates the disk and is smooth over a smaller punctured disk.*\n\n4.  *For each vertical prime $E$ on the downstairs order resolution, some prime on $Z_N$ dominates $E$.*\n\n*Consequently <a href=\"#hodge:FF\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"hodge:FF\">[hodge:FF]</a> applies to this model, and its top eigenline tests the extension of the original line as in Lemma <a href=\"#hodge:birational\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"hodge:birational\">8</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-orders-diskmodels",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the local root family of Section <a href=\"#hodge:rootconstruction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"hodge:rootconstruction\">3.1</a> and a resolved transverse disk downstairs. On the punctured disk it is a modification of the pulled-back root family.*\n\n2.  *The horizontal removed divisor $H_N$, defined by the reduced full inverse image of the original removed divisor on the punctured disk and then closed up, is SNC. Its support and the full central fiber have joint SNC support.*\n\n3.  *Every stratum of $(Z_N,H_N)$ dominates the disk and is smooth over a smaller punctured disk.*\n\n4.  *For each vertical prime $E$ on the downstairs order resolution, some prime on $Z_N$ dominates $E$.*\n\n*Consequently <a href=\"#hodge:FF\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"hodge:FF\">[hodge:FF]</a> applies to this model, and its top eigenline tests the extension of the original line as in Lemma <a href=\"#hodge:birational\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"hodge:birational\">8</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.5"
        }

      "id": "stmt-orders-diskmodels",
      "kind": "construction",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-orders-diskmodels",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the local root family of Section <a href=\"#hodge:rootconstruction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"hodge:rootconstruction\">3.1</a> and a resolved transverse disk downstairs. For every sufficiently divisible power substitution $t=u^N$, there is a smooth equivariant Kähler model over $\\Delta_u$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *It retains every component of the generic root cover and maps to the downstairs order resolution. On the punctured disk it is a modification of the pulled-back root family.*\n\n2.  *The horizontal removed divisor $H_N$, defined by the reduced full inverse image of the original removed divisor on the punctured disk and then closed up, is SNC. Its support and the full central fiber have joint SNC support.*\n\n3.  *Every stratum of $(Z_N,H_N)$ dominates the disk and is smooth over a smaller punctured disk.*\n\n4.  *For each vertical prime $E$ on the downstairs order resolution, some prime on $Z_N$ dominates $E$.*\n\n*Consequently <a href=\"#hodge:FF\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"hodge:FF\">[hodge:FF]</a> applies to this model, and its top eigenline tests the extension of the original line as in Lemma <a href=\"#hodge:birational\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"hodge:birational\">8</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.5"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-orders-diskmodels"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-orders-order",
        "exact_quote": "The section $\\tau$ is meromorphic in that extended line. The normalization divides an ordinary order after $t=u^N$ by $N$. At the displayed order, the corrected section generates the extension after clearing its denominator.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.5"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-orders-residuenorm",
        "exact_quote": "The expression <a href=\"#orders:residuenormformula\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"orders:residuenormformula\">[orders:residuenormformula]</a> is a positive finite norm, homogeneous of degree one, on the volume line. Let $a$ clear the indicated powers. Along any disk in $P$ whose punctured part lies in $P^\\circ$, after a sufficiently divisible power substitution, a nonvanishing local frame of $$r^*\\mathcal O_S(amM_S)$$ and its dual have at most powers-of-log growth in the $a$-th tensor power of this norm and its dual norm. No Kähler assumption on $P$ is required.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spread-marked",
        "exact_quote": "There are a smooth compact Kähler manifold $\\Sigma$, a proper surjection $\\rho:\\Sigma\\to S$, and a dense open $\\Sigma^\\circ\\subseteq\\rho^{-1}(U)$ with the following data:*\n\n1.  *a proper smooth family $a:\\mathscr A^\\circ\\to\\Sigma^\\circ$ with connected fibers and a holomorphic section;*\n\n2.  *an effective relative SNC rational boundary $D_{\\mathscr A}$ with coefficients in $[0,1)$;*\n\n3.  *an identification $$a_*\\mathcal O_{\\mathscr A^\\circ}\n           \\bigl(m(K_{\\mathscr A^\\circ/\\Sigma^\\circ}+D_{\\mathscr A})\\bigr)\n     \\simeq \\rho^*\\mathcal G_m.$$ The relative log-pluri bundle itself is the pullback of this line.         "anchor": "stmt-spread-fiber-splitting",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(F,D)$ be a smooth connected compact Kähler pair, with effective SNC rational boundary of coefficients less than one, and suppose that $m(K_F+D)$ is trivial.       "exact_statement": "Let $(F,D)$ be a smooth connected compact Kähler pair, with effective SNC rational boundary of coefficients less than one, and suppose that $m(K_F+D)$ is trivial. There is a finite étale cover $\\nu:J\\to F$ and an isomorphism of pairs $$\\label{spread:fiber-product}\n (J,\\nu^*D)\\simeq (Q,C)\\times T_1\\times\\cdots\\times T_j,$$ where $(Q,C)$ is a smooth projective klt SNC pair, and each $T_i$ is a positive-dimensional complex torus or an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold. The boundary is entirely on $Q$. A point is allowed for $Q$, and $j=0$ is allowed. Moreover, $m(K_Q+C)$ and all $mK_{T_i}$ are trivial.",
      "explanation": "A finite étale cover splits a klt log-pluricanonically trivial Kähler fiber into a projective boundary-bearing factor and boundary-free torus or symplectic factors.",

          "source_id": "PM-033.5"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spread-finite-covers",
        "exact_quote": "Let $g:E^\\circ\\to B^\\circ$ be a proper smooth holomorphic map of connected complex manifolds, with connected compact fibers and a section. Suppose $B^\\circ$ is a dense open in a smooth compact Kähler manifold $B$, and the family has a smooth compact Kähler extension $E\\to B$. Then all finite connected étale covers of individual fibers occur in countably many families, each obtained after a finite étale base cover of $B^\\circ$. Each resulting base and total space admit smooth compact Kähler extensions preserving the open families. The same holds with pulled-back relative SNC boundary data.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spread-finite-covers",

Then all finite connected étale covers of individual fibers occur in countably many families, each obtained after a finite étale base cover of $B^\\circ$. Each resulting base and total space admit smooth compact Kähler extensions preserving the open families. The same holds with pulled-back relative SNC boundary data.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.5"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spread-constructible",
        "exact_quote": "Let $q:Z\\to T$ be a proper holomorphic map of compact complex spaces. The image of a constructible subset of $Z$ is constructible in $T$. Consequently, any finite combination of incidence, emptiness, surjectivity, and injectivity conditions on fibers of compact analytic diagrams is constructible.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spread-constructible",
          "exact_quote": "Let $q:Z\\to T$ be a proper holomorphic map of compact complex spaces. The image of a constructible subset of $Z$ is constructible in $T$. Consequently, any finite combination of incidence, emptiness, surjectivity, and injectivity conditions on fibers of compact analytic diagrams is constructible.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $q:Z\\to T$ be a proper holomorphic map of compact complex spaces. The image of a constructible subset of $Z$ is constructible in $T$.       "explanation": "Proper holomorphic images preserve constructibility in compact complex spaces and allow finite combinations of fiberwise incidence conditions.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spread-product",
        "exact_quote": "After a proper surjection $r:P\\to S$, with $P$ a smooth compact connected manifold, there is a dense open $P^\\circ\\subseteq r^{-1}(U)$ on which the marked klt family has a finite étale cover and a product decomposition $$\\label{spread:family-product}\n (\\mathscr J,D_{\\mathscr J})\n \\simeq\n (\\mathscr Q,C)\\times_{P^\\circ}\n \\mathscr T_1\\times_{P^\\circ}\\cdots\\times_{P^\\circ}\\mathscr T_j .$$ Here all families are proper and smooth with connected fibers. They have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The pair $(\\mathscr Q,C)$ is a projective family of effective klt SNC pairs. It is embedded in $P^\\circ\\times\\mathbb P^N$ and is the pullback of universal marked families by the restriction of a holomorphic map from $P$ to a fixed product of projective Hilbert schemes.*\n\n2.  *Each $\\mathscr T_i$ is a family of submanifolds of a fixed compact Kähler manifold $V$, of positive dimension $n_i$. Its cohomological local systems have their integral lattices modulo torsion and flat real polarizations. Its top Hodge line has rank one and is given by $$\\lambda_i\n     =(\\pi_i)_*\\omega_{\\mathscr T_i/P^\\circ}\n     =F^{n_i}R^{n_i}(\\pi_i)_*\\mathbb C$$ Each index is of one of the following two types: $$\\begin{array}{ll}\n     \\text{torus type:}&\n     h^{1,0}(T_i)=n_i,\\quad\n     \\bigwedge^{n_i}F^1R^1(\\pi_i)_*\\mathbb C\n           \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\\lambda_i;\\\\[2mm]\n     \\text{symplectic type:}&\n     n_i\\ \\text{even},\\quad h^{2,0}(T_i)=1,\\quad\n     \\bigl(F^2R^2(\\pi_i)_*\\mathbb C\\bigr)^{\\otimes n_i/2}\n           \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\\lambda_i .\n     \\end{array}$$ The displayed maps are cup products on the underlying variations.*\n\n\n3.  *On every fiber, $m(K_Q+C)$ and $mK_{T_i}$ are trivial. With $$\\mathcal G_{m,Q}\n       =(\\pi_Q)_*\\mathcal O_{\\mathscr Q}\n                      \\bigl(m(K_{\\mathscr Q/P^\\circ}+C)\\bigr),$$ there is an identification of actual holomorphic line bundles $$\\label{spread:volume-line}\n     r^*\\mathcal G_m\n       \\simeq \\mathcal G_{m,Q}\\otimes\n                       \\bigotimes_i\\lambda_i^{\\otimes m}.$$*\n\n\n4.  *If the finite cover in <a href=\"#spread:family-product\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spread:family-product\">[spread:family-product]</a> has degree $e$, the norm in <a href=\"#spread:residue-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spread:residue-norm\">[spread:residue-norm]</a> satisfies $$\\label{spread:norm-product}\n     \\|s\\|_{\\mathrm{res}}\n       =c\\,\\|s_Q\\|_{Q,\\mathrm{res}}\\,\n                       \\prod_i\\|\\omega_i\\|_{\\mathrm{Hdg}}^m\n     \\quad\\text{when}\\quad\n     s\\longleftrightarrow\n           s_Q\\otimes\\bigotimes_i\\omega_i^{\\otimes m}.$$ Here $c>0$ is constant; with compatible volume conventions, $c=e^{-m/2}$. The norm on the left gives generators and their duals of sufficiently divisible powers of $r^*(mM_S)$ the two-sided logarithmic bounds of Lemma <a href=\"#spread:residue-growth\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spread:residue-growth\">16</a>.*\n\n*These assertions are preserved on the good open under further smooth compact auxiliary base changes. Neither generic finiteness of $r$ nor a global Kähler form on $P$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spread-product",
          "exact_quote": "After a proper surjection $r:P\\to S$, with $P$ a smooth compact connected manifold, there is a dense open $P^\\circ\\subseteq r^{-1}(U)$ on which the marked klt family has a finite étale cover and a product decomposition $$\\label{spread:family-product}\n (\\mathscr J,D_{\\mathscr J})\n \\simeq\n (\\mathscr Q,C)\\times_{P^\\circ}\n \\mathscr T_1\\times_{P^\\circ}\\cdots\\times_{P^\\circ}\\mathscr T_j .$$ Here all families are proper and smooth with connected fibers. They have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The pair $(\\mathscr Q,C)$ is a projective family of effective klt SNC pairs. It is embedded in $P^\\circ\\times\\mathbb P^N$ and is the pullback of universal marked families by the restriction of a holomorphic map from $P$ to a fixed product of projective Hilbert schemes.*\n\n2.  *Each $\\mathscr T_i$ is a family of submanifolds of a fixed compact Kähler manifold $V$, of positive dimension $n_i$. Its cohomological local systems have their integral lattices modulo torsion and flat real polarizations. Its top Hodge line has rank one and is given by $$\\lambda_i\n     =(\\pi_i)_*\\omega_{\\mathscr T_i/P^\\circ}\n     =F^{n_i}R^{n_i}(\\pi_i)_*\\mathbb C$$ Each index is of one of the following two types: $$\\begin{array}{ll}\n     \\text{torus type:}&\n     h^{1,0}(T_i)=n_i,\\quad\n     \\bigwedge^{n_i}F^1R^1(\\pi_i)_*\\mathbb C\n           \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\\lambda_i;\\\\[2mm]\n     \\text{symplectic type:}&\n     n_i\\ \\text{even},\\quad h^{2,0}(T_i)=1,\\quad\n     \\bigl(F^2R^2(\\pi_i)_*\\mathbb C\\bigr)^{\\otimes n_i/2}\n           \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\\lambda_i .\n     \\end{array}$$ The displayed maps are cup products on the underlying variations.*\n\n\n3.  *On every fiber, $m(K_Q+C)$ and $mK_{T_i}$ are trivial. With $$\\mathcal G_{m,Q}\n       =(\\pi_Q)_*\\mathcal O_{\\mathscr Q}\n                      \\bigl(m(K_{\\mathscr Q/P^\\circ}+C)\\bigr),$$ there is an identification of actual holomorphic line bundles $$\\label{spread:volume-line}\n     r^*\\mathcal G_m\n       \\simeq \\mathcal G_{m,Q}\\otimes\n                       \\bigotimes_i\\lambda_i^{\\otimes m}.$$*\n\n\n4.  *If the finite cover in <a href=\"#spread:family-product\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spread:family-product\">[spread:family-product]</a> has degree $e$, the norm in <a href=\"#spread:residue-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spread:residue-norm\">[spread:residue-norm]</a> satisfies $$\\label{spread:norm-product}\n     \\|s\\|_{\\mathrm{res}}\n       =c\\,\\|s_Q\\|_{Q,\\mathrm{res}}\\,\n                       \\prod_i\\|\\omega_i\\|_{\\mathrm{Hdg}}^m\n     \\quad\\text{when}\\quad\n     s\\longleftrightarrow\n           s_Q\\otimes\\bigotimes_i\\omega_i^{\\otimes m}.$$ Here $c>0$ is constant; with compatible volume conventions, $c=e^{-m/2}$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-compare-semiample",
        "exact_quote": "Use the notation of Proposition <a href=\"#spread:product\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spread:product\">20</a>, and write $P^\\circ\\subset P$ for its good open set. After a proper modification $\\pi:P'\\to P$, with $P'$ smooth compact, and a further shrinking of $P^\\circ$, there are a positive integer $a$, a globally generated holomorphic line bundle $\\mathcal H_Q$ on $P'$, and a specified isomorphism $$\\label{compare:open-identification}\n \\mathcal H_Q|_{\\pi^{-1}(P^\\circ)}\n \\simeq\n \\bigl(\\pi^*\\mathcal G_{m,Q}\\bigr)^{\\otimes a}\n       |_{\\pi^{-1}(P^\\circ)} .$$ Transport to the left the $a$-th power of the volume norm $$N_{m,Q}(s)=\n \\left(\\int_{Q_x}|s|^{2/m}\\right)^{m/2}.$$ For any holomorphic disk in $P'$ whose punctured disk lies in the good open, a local generator of the pulled-back $\\mathcal H_Q$ and its dual have norm bounded by powers of $1+|\\log|t||$, after a finite power substitution if necessary.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "exact_quote": "Use the notation of Proposition <a href=\"#spread:product\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spread:product\">20</a>, and write $P^\\circ\\subset P$ for its good open set.           "source_id": "PM-033.5"

          "anchor": "stmt-compare-semiample",
          "exact_quote": "Use the notation of Proposition <a href=\"#spread:product\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spread:product\">20</a>, and write $P^\\circ\\subset P$ for its good open set. After a proper modification $\\pi:P'\\to P$, with $P'$ smooth compact, and a further shrinking of $P^\\circ$, there are a positive integer $a$, a globally generated holomorphic line bundle $\\mathcal H_Q$ on $P'$, and a specified isomorphism $$\\label{compare:open-identification}\n \\mathcal H_Q|_{\\pi^{-1}(P^\\circ)}\n \\simeq\n \\bigl(\\pi^*\\mathcal G_{m,Q}\\bigr)^{\\otimes a}\n       |_{\\pi^{-1}(P^\\circ)} .$$ Transport to the left the $a$-th power of the volume norm $$N_{m,Q}(s)=\n \\left(\\int_{Q_x}|s|^{2/m}\\right)^{m/2}.$$ For any holomorphic disk in $P'$ whose punctured disk lies in the good open, a local generator of the pulled-back $\\mathcal H_Q$ and its dual have norm bounded by powers of $1+|\\log|t||$, after a finite power substitution if necessary.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.5"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-compare-semiample"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-periods-rationalization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(\\mathbb V_{\\mathbb Z},F^\\bullet)$ be an integral pure variation with a flat real polarization $B$. There is a flat real automorphism $C$ of $\\mathbb V_{\\mathbb R}$ such that $(\\mathbb V_{\\mathbb Z},C^{-1}F^\\bullet)$ is polarized by a rational form $B'$, and $$B'(x,y)=B(Cx,Cy).$$ Consequently the new and original variations are isomorphic as real polarized variations, and their holomorphic Hodge bundles are isomorphic. Their canonical Hodge extensions are also identified wherever local monodromy is unipotent. After multiplying $B'$ by a positive integer, its values on $\\mathbb V_{\\mathbb Z}$ are integral.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-periods-lefschetz",
        "exact_quote": "Let $t:\\mathcal T\\to B^\\circ$ be a smooth proper family of connected compact complex manifolds of dimension $n$ over a complex manifold $B^\\circ$, and suppose a closed real $(1,1)$-form on $\\mathcal T$ restricts to a Kähler form on every fiber. Its restriction class $\\kappa$ is flat. The cohomological variations, with their integral lattices modulo torsion, are real-polarizable. In particular:*\n\n1.  *on $H^1$, a polarization is $$B_1(\\alpha,\\beta)=\\int_{\\mathcal T_b}\n                      \\alpha\\wedge\\beta\\wedge\\kappa^{n-1};$$*\n\n\n2.  *if $n\\geq2$, write $H^2_{\\mathbb R}=P^2_{\\mathbb R}\\oplus\\mathbb R\\kappa$, where $P^2_{\\mathbb R}=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ker}}(\\kappa^{n-1}\\cup-)$. For $\\alpha=\\alpha_0+a\\kappa$ and $\\beta=\\beta_0+b\\kappa$, a full weight-two polarization is $$\\label{periods:full-h2}\n     B_2(\\alpha,\\beta)=\n     \\int_{\\mathcal T_b}\\alpha_0\\wedge\\beta_0\\wedge\\kappa^{n-2}\n     -ab\\int_{\\mathcal T_b}\\kappa^n.$$ If $h^{2,0}=1$, this real form has signature $(2,h^{1,1})$.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-periods-extensions",
        "exact_quote": "Let a pure real-polarizable integral variation be defined on the complement of an SNC divisor in a complex manifold, with unipotent local monodromy. Canonical Hodge extensions commute with tensor products, exterior powers, duals, and holomorphic pullbacks from smooth manifolds with SNC boundary, whose boundary complements map into the original complement. This includes disks whose punctured disks map into that complement. A morphism of pure real variations induces an isomorphism between its coimage and image on these extensions. In particular, an isomorphism of highest Hodge lines induced by such a morphism remains an isomorphism after extension.*\n\n\n*If $\\mathcal H$ is a highest Hodge line, $\\gamma:\\Delta\\to B$ is a disk with $\\gamma(\\Delta^*)$ in the open set, and $e$ is a nowhere-zero local section of $\\gamma^*\\overline{\\mathcal H}$, there are constants $c>0$ and $a\\geq0$ such that, for $0<|z|$ sufficiently small, $$\\label{periods:logbounds}\n c^{-1}(1+|\\log|z||)^{-a}\n \\ \\leq\\ \\|e(z)\\|\\ \\leq\\\n c(1+|\\log|z||)^a.$$ The same assertion holds for its powers and their duals.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-periods-extensions",
          "exact_quote": "Let a pure real-polarizable integral variation be defined on the complement of an SNC divisor in a complex manifold, with unipotent local monodromy. Canonical Hodge extensions commute with tensor products, exterior powers, duals, and holomorphic pullbacks from smooth manifolds with SNC boundary, whose boundary complements map into the original complement. This includes disks whose punctured disks map into that complement. A morphism of pure real variations induces an isomorphism between its coimage and image on these extensions. In particular, an isomorphism of highest Hodge lines induced by such a morphism remains an isomorphism after extension.*\n\n\n*If $\\mathcal H$ is a highest Hodge line, $\\gamma:\\Delta\\to B$ is a disk with $\\gamma(\\Delta^*)$ in the open set, and $e$ is a nowhere-zero local section of $\\gamma^*\\overline{\\mathcal H}$, there are constants $c>0$ and $a\\geq0$ such that, for $0<|z|$ sufficiently small, $$\\label{periods:logbounds}\n c^{-1}(1+|\\log|z||)^{-a}\n \\ \\leq\\ \\|e(z)\\|\\ \\leq\\\n c(1+|\\log|z||)^a.$$ The same assertion holds for its powers and their duals.",
          "source_id": "PM-033.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let a pure real-polarizable integral variation be defined on the complement of an SNC divisor in a complex manifold, with unipotent local monodromy. This includes disks whose punctured disks map into that complement. A morphism of pure real variations induces an isomorphism between its coimage and image on these extensions.       "explanation": "Unipotent canonical Hodge extensions preserve tensor operations, pullbacks and pure morphism images, with two-sided highest-line norm estimates.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-periods-griffiths",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a smooth compact complex manifold, let $D$ be an SNC divisor, and let $\\mathbb V$ be a real-polarizable integral variation on $B^\\circ=B\\setminus D$. Suppose its Hodge numbers are either $$w=1,\\quad h^{1,0}=h^{0,1}=g,\n  \\qquad\\text{or}\\qquad\n  w=2,\\quad h^{2,0}=h^{0,2}=1.$$ After a finite cover of $B^\\circ$, extended and resolved over $B$, the canonical extension of its Griffiths line is semiample. The resulting base is smooth and compact and its boundary can be taken SNC.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-periods-griffiths",
          "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a smooth compact complex manifold, let $D$ be an SNC divisor, and let $\\mathbb V$ be a real-polarizable integral variation on $B^\\circ=B\\setminus D$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-periods-semiample",
        "exact_quote": "Let $r:P\\to S$ and the smooth families $t_i:T_i\\to P^\\circ$ be as in Proposition <a href=\"#spread:product\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spread:product\">20</a>, with $n_i=\\dim T_{i,u}>0$, and put $$\\mathcal H_i=F^{n_i}\\bigl(R^{n_i}(t_i)_*\\mathbb C\\otimes\\mathcal O_{P^\\circ}\\bigr).$$ Thus $\\mathcal H_i$ is the highest line denoted $\\lambda_i$ in Proposition <a href=\"#spread:product\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spread:product\">20</a>; the symbol $\\lambda$ above denotes the Griffiths line. After a proper generically finite surjection $\\pi:P'\\to P$, obtained by extending a finite *étale* cover of the good open and resolving, there are semiample holomorphic line bundles $\\overline{\\mathcal H}_i$ on the smooth compact manifold $P'$ whose specified restrictions are $\\pi^*\\mathcal H_i$.*\n\n*The boundary may be taken SNC. Along any disk in $P'$ whose punctured disk lies in the good open, a generator of $\\overline{\\mathcal H}_i$ and its inverse have at most powers-of-logarithm growth in the volume norm <a href=\"#periods:volume\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"periods:volume\">[periods:volume]</a> and its dual. The same is true for all positive powers, including the powers used in the product-volume identification of Proposition <a href=\"#spread:product\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spread:product\">20</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-033.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "quantifiers": [
        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-descent-unit",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be a complex manifold, $D$ a simple normal crossing divisor, and $P^\\circ=P\\setminus D$. Let $E_1,E_2$ be holomorphic line bundles on $P$, equipped on $P^\\circ$ with Hermitian norms, and let $$\\phi:E_1|_{P^\\circ}\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }E_2|_{P^\\circ}$$ be a specified holomorphic isomorphism. Assume the following near the smooth locus of each component of $D$. In a coordinate polydisk $(t,z)$ with $D=(t=0)$, choose nonvanishing holomorphic frames $e_1,e_2$. For a dense set of centers $z$, their norms and dual norms along the parallel punctured disks satisfy bounds by powers of $1+|\\log|t||$. The constants and powers may depend on the disk. Assume also that $\\phi$ compares the two norms by factors bounded above and below on each such disk. It is enough for these bounds to hold after a finite power substitution on each disk. Then $\\phi$ extends uniquely to a holomorphic isomorphism $E_1\\simeq E_2$ on $P$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Modification** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A *modification* is a proper bimeromorphic holomorphic map.
- **Rational Picard group** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Write $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)_{\mathbb Q}=\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Q$, using additive notation.
- **Threshold over a base prime** ([source](#eq:threshold)): For a prime divisor $P\subset X'$, put $$\label{eq:threshold} t_P=\inf_{E\mapsto P} \frac{a(E;Y',\Delta')}{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E((f')^*P)}.$$
- **Threshold discriminant and moduli trace** ([source](#eq:moduli)): Define $$\label{eq:moduli} B_{X'}=\sum_P(1-t_P)P,\qquad M_{X'}=\mu^*L-K_{X'}-B_{X'}\ \in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X')_{\mathbb Q}.$$
- **Moduli rational b-line bundle** ([source](#eq:moduli)): The family $\mathbf M=(M_{X'})$ is the *moduli rational b-line bundle*.
- **Log discrepancy** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For a smooth model $h:V\to Y$, log discrepancy is normalized by $$a(E;Y,\Delta) =1+\operatorname{coeff}_E(K_{V/Y}-h^*\Delta).$$
- **Sub-pair** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A *sub-pair* allows negative boundary coefficients; it is sub-log-canonical when these numbers are nonnegative.
- **Constructible subset** ([source](#pm-h-23)): We call a subset of a compact complex space *constructible* if it is a finite union of differences of closed analytic subsets.
- **Integral variation** ([source](#periods:sign)): An integral variation in this section consists of a local system $\mathbb V_{\mathbb Z}$ of finite free abelian groups and a holomorphic Hodge filtration on $E=\mathbb V_{\mathbb Z}\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathcal O$, satisfying opposedness and Griffiths transversality.
- **Real polarization** ([source](#periods:sign)): A real polarization is a flat nondegenerate real bilinear form on $\mathbb V_{\mathbb R}$ satisfying the Hodge–Riemann relations.
- **Griffiths line** ([source](#pm-h-28)): For an effective pure variation of weight $w$, we use the Griffiths line $$\lambda=\bigotimes_{p=1}^{w}\det F^pE.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| threshold-minimum | Threshold over a base prime | = | $\min_{E\mapsto P}\frac{1-\delta_E}{a_E}$ | Vertical primes dominating the general point of the fixed base prime. |
| root-cover-order | Order at a strict horizontal covering prime | = | $j(1-\delta)-1$ | Ramification index and boundary coefficient of the covering prime. |
| normalized-volume-order | Normalized Hodge order of the root volume | = | $\min_E\frac{l_E+1-a_E}{a_E}$ | Central-fiber primes, after clearing denominators and making monodromy unipotent. |
| threshold-trace-order | Hodge order along a base prime | = | $t_P-1$ | Every base prime, allowing arbitrary rational vertical coefficients. |
| symplectic-top-line-power | Tensor exponent producing the symplectic highest line | = | $n_i/2$ | Positive even fiber dimension and the specified cup-product identification. |
| section-descent-exponent | Exponent yielding global generation downstairs | = | $nd$ | A globally generated pulled-back power and the generic degree of the finite Stein factor. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **threshold** [claim](#stmt-prelim-threshold) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prelim-threshold): Joint simple normal crossing discrepancy calculation -> Rational threshold minimum and finite discriminant support
- **residue** [claim](#stmt-hodge-residue) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hodge-residue): Root eigenspace and iterated residue -> Pure highest line carrying the specified volume
- **trace** [claim](#stmt-orders-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-orders-trace): Disk extension tests including the base Jacobian -> Actual threshold moduli extension
- **stabilization** [claim](#stmt-orders-bdescent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-orders-bdescent): Valuation comparison on further base models -> Pullback equality of rational moduli lines
- **marked** [claim](#stmt-spread-marked) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spread-marked): Deepest-stratum adjunction and diagonal marking -> Marked connected klt family with a section
- **product** [claim](#stmt-spread-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spread-product): Finite covers, compact parameter spaces and Baire category -> Compact proper surjection carrying the product family
- **projective** [claim](#stmt-compare-semiample) / [proof](#proof-stmt-compare-semiample): Projective comparison and algebraic b-semiampleness -> Globally generated projective-factor extension
- **periods** [claim](#stmt-periods-semiample) / [proof](#proof-stmt-periods-semiample): Real polarization transport and arithmetic period compactification -> Semiample highest-line extensions with logarithmic norm bounds
- **extension** [claim](#stmt-descent-unit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-descent-unit): Laurent coefficients and Hartogs extension -> Global isomorphism of actual line bundles
- **descent** [claim](#stmt-descent-semiample) / [proof](#proof-stmt-descent-semiample): Stein factorization and multiplicative analytic norms -> Global generation of a common power on the original base model

It is embedded in $P^\\circ\\times\\mathbb P^N$ and is the pullback of universal marked families by the restriction of a holomorphic map from $P$ to a fixed product of projective Hilbert schemes.*\n\n2.  *Each $\\mathscr T_i$ is a family of submanifolds of a fixed compact Kähler manifold $V$, of positive dimension $n_i$. Its cohomological local systems have their integral lattices modulo torsion and flat real polarizations. Its top Hodge line has rank one and is given by $$\\lambda_i\n     =(\\pi_i)_*\\omega_{\\mathscr T_i/P^\\circ}\n     =F^{n_i}R^{n_i}(\\pi_i)_*\\mathbb C$$ Each index is of one of the following two types: $$\\begin{array}{ll}\n     \\text{torus type:}&\n     h^{1,0}(T_i)=n_i,\\quad\n     \\bigwedge^{n_i}F^1R^1(\\pi_i)_*\\mathbb C\n           \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\\lambda_i;\\\\[2mm]\n     \\text{symplectic type:}&\n     n_i\\ \\text{even},\\quad h^{2,0}(T_i)=1,\\quad\n     \\bigl(F^2R^2(\\pi_i)_*\\mathbb C\\bigr)^{\\otimes n_i/2}\n           \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\\lambda_i .\n     \\end{array}$$ The displayed maps are cup products on the underlying variations.*\n\n\n3.  *On every fiber, $m(K_Q+C)$ and $mK_{T_i}$ are trivial. With $$\\mathcal G_{m,Q}\n       =(\\pi_Q)_*\\mathcal O_{\\mathscr Q}\n                      \\bigl(m(K_{\\mathscr Q/P^\\circ}+C)\\bigr),$$ there is an identification of actual holomorphic line bundles $$\\label{spread:volume-line}\n     r^*\\mathcal G_m\n       \\simeq \\mathcal G_{m,Q}\\otimes\n                       \\bigotimes_i\\lambda_i^{\\otimes m}.$$*\n\n\n4.  *If the finite cover in <a href=\"#spread:family-product\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spread:family-product\">[spread:family-product]</a> has degree $e$, the norm in <a href=\"#spread:residue-norm\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spread:residue-norm\">[spread:residue-norm]</a> satisfies $$\\label{spread:norm-product}\n     \\|s\\|_{\\mathrm{res}}\n       =c\\,\\|s_Q\\|_{Q,\\mathrm{res}}\\,\n                       \\prod_i\\|\\omega_i\\|_{\\mathrm{Hdg}}^m\n     \\quad\\text{when}\\quad\n     s\\longleftrightarrow\n           s_Q\\otimes\\bigotimes_i\\omega_i^{\\otimes m}.$$ Here $c>0$ is constant; with compatible volume conventions, $c=e^{-m/2}$. The norm on the left gives generators and their duals of sufficiently divisible powers of $r^*(mM_S)$ the two-sided logarithmic bounds of Lemma <a href=\"#spread:residue-growth\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spread:residue-growth\">16</a>.*\n\n*These assertions are preserved on the good open under further smooth compact auxiliary base changes. Neither generic finiteness of $r$ nor a global Kähler form on $P$ is required.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **threshold-minimum**: The threshold is the minimum of the discrepancy ratios on the joint SNC model; further exceptional valuations do not change it.
- **volume-order**: The normalized Hodge order of the specified root volume equals the threshold minus one.
- **higher-model-descent**: The actual rational moduli line pulls back on every further smooth compact Kähler modification.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **orbifold-substitution**: Replace the threshold discriminant by an orbifold multiplicity divisor. -> The resulting divisor need not define the moduli line being proved semiample.
- **numerical-substitution**: Infer holomorphic global generation from numerical semipositivity alone. -> A numerical identification does not preserve the required actual line bundle or its specified open isomorphism.
- **pointwise-splitting**: Use a product decomposition of an individual fiber as a family decomposition. -> Pointwise splitting alone does not supply the family comparison needed for semiampleness.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The threshold minimum on an already supplied joint SNC model is a finite rational calculation. The code checks this local calculation and the arithmetic order shift; constructing a resolution, identifying the Hodge extension, and proving global semiampleness remain outside its coverage.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON batch of nonempty lists of [delta_numerator, delta_denominator, multiplicity].
# PRECONDITIONS: Supplied joint SNC data; integers, positive denominators and multiplicities; at most 256 cases and 64 rows per case.
# OUTPUTS: JSON [threshold numerator, denominator, shifted-order numerator, denominator] per case, or INVALID.
# TIME: Linear in the supplied row count, with exact rational arithmetic costs.
# SPACE: Linear in the supplied batch size and rational bit lengths.
# ARITHMETIC: Certified exact python-flint fmpq arithmetic; no floating point.
# FAILURE: INVALID for malformed or out-of-contract input; geometry outside supplied SNC data is not decided.
import json
import sys
from flint import fmpq

def compute(batch):
    if not isinstance(batch, list) or not 1 <= len(batch) <= 256:
        raise ValueError()
    answers = []
    for case in batch:
        if not isinstance(case, list) or not 1 <= len(case) <= 64:
            raise ValueError()
        ratios = []
        for row in case:
            if not isinstance(row, list) or len(row) != 3 or any(type(v) is not int for v in row):
                raise ValueError()
            numerator, denominator, multiplicity = row
            if denominator <= 0 or multiplicity <= 0:
                raise ValueError()
            delta = fmpq(numerator, denominator)
            ratios.append((1 - delta) / multiplicity)
        threshold = min(ratios)
        order = threshold - 1
        answers.append([int(threshold.numerator), int(threshold.denominator), int(order.numerator), int(order.denominator)])
    return answers

try:
    print(json.dumps(compute(json.loads(sys.stdin.read())), separators=(',', ':')))
except (ValueError, TypeError, OverflowError):
    print('INVALID')
```

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The main conclusion requires a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers between smooth compact connected Kähler manifolds, an effective rational SNC boundary in the stated coefficient range, and the rational adjoint pullback identity. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to noncompact spaces, disconnected fibers, or boundary data outside the stated assumptions.']
- **L-boundary** (hypothesis): Effectivity and the coefficient bounds apply to the original boundary; crepant exceptional coefficients on higher source models may be negative. Blocked use: ['Requiring every crepant source coefficient to remain effective.', 'Discarding negative exceptional coefficients when computing thresholds.']
- **L-threshold** (hypothesis): The moduli trace uses the log-canonical-threshold discriminant; an orbifold multiplicity divisor cannot replace it without the required identification. Blocked use: ['Substituting the weighted inf-multiplicity divisor for the threshold discriminant.']
- **L-actual-bundles** (hypothesis): The conclusion concerns actual holomorphic line bundles and global generation; numerical semipositivity does not supply the required bundle isomorphism. Blocked use: ['Treating numerical positivity as a proof of global generation.']
- **L-model-input** (formal_scope): The threshold minimum is an arithmetic calculation after a joint SNC resolution and the crepant coefficients and positive pullback orders have been supplied. Arithmetic evaluation does not establish the existence or completeness of that geometric input. Blocked use: ['Certifying a threshold from an arbitrary incomplete list of valuations.', 'Treating finite rational arithmetic as verification of the geometric theorem.']
- **L-existential** (nonconstructive): The proof gives existence of a suitable modification and a positive globally generating multiple; it does not provide an effective universal bound for that multiple. Blocked use: ['Claiming an effective universal generation exponent or a terminating construction of the modification from the theorem alone.']
- **L-baire** (nonconstructive): Selection of a dominating compact parameter closure uses a countable collection and Baire category, rather than a finite search procedure supplied by the paper. Blocked use: ['Treating the family-spreading argument as an enumerated terminating algorithm.']
- **L-pointwise** (hypothesis): A pointwise product decomposition alone does not prove semiampleness; the proof requires a compact dominating auxiliary family with compatible volume identifications. Blocked use: ['Inferring semiampleness directly from separate fiber decompositions.']
- **L-auxiliary** (counterexample_scope): The compact auxiliary base used for generation is distinct from the compact Kähler modification on which the moduli trace stabilizes. Blocked use: ['Identifying the auxiliary parameter space with the stabilizing modification.', 'Assuming the auxiliary surjection must be generically finite or its source Kähler.']
- **L-rationalization** (counterexample_scope): Rationalization changes the Hodge filtration on the integral local system while preserving the holomorphic Hodge bundles; it does not rationally polarize the original Hodge structure of a nonprojective fiber. Blocked use: ['Inferring projectivity or a rational polarization of the original fiber from filtration transport.']
- **L-degenerate** (edge_case): The theorem includes a point base and relative dimension zero; positive dimensions are imposed only for the remaining proof. Blocked use: ['Excluding the degenerate cases from the theorem.']
- **L-extension** (hypothesis): Extension of the specified open isomorphism requires control of frames and dual frames and comparison of the norms; a one-sided bound cannot replace these hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Extending an arbitrary open isomorphism from a one-sided growth estimate alone.']
- **L-local-constants** (approximation): Logarithmic growth constants and powers may depend on the disk; the stated estimates do not give uniform numerical constants across all disks. Blocked use: ['Using the logarithmic bounds as a uniform numerical error estimate over the entire base.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the compact log-smooth Kähler case of b-semiampleness. Let $`f:Y\to X`$ be a surjective holomorphic map with connected fibers between smooth compact connected Kähler manifolds, and let Δ be an effective rational divisor with simple normal crossing support and coefficients in $`[0,1]`$, with $`K_Y+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb Q}f^*L`$ for $`L\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}\nolimits (X)_{\mathbb Q}`$. There is a smooth compact Kähler modification $`S\to X`$ for which the threshold-moduli line satisfies $`M_{S_1}=\nu^*M_S`$ in $`\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}\nolimits (S_1)_{\mathbb Q}`$ for every smooth compact Kähler modification $`\nu:S_1\to S`$, and some positive multiple of <i>M</i><sub><i>S</i></sub> is represented by a holomorphic line bundle generated by global sections. Horizontal components of coefficient one are allowed; neither projectivity nor a Campana orbifold Iitaka hypothesis is assumed.

<a id="sec:intro"></a>
### Introduction

The canonical bundle formula separates the canonical geometry of a fibration into a discriminant, which measures singularities over divisors, and a moduli part, which measures variation. The b-semiampleness conjecture predicts that a suitable multiple of the moduli part defines a morphism on a birational model of the base. Hodge-theoretic semipositivity underlies this formula (Kawamata 1981) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Kawamata81](#ref-Kawamata81)]. Ambro established birational stabilization and positivity of the moduli part in the generic-klt setting (Ambro 2004, 2005) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Ambro04](#ref-Ambro04), [Ambro05](#ref-Ambro05)]; Fujino–Gongyo proved b-nefness and abundance for projective lc-trivial fibrations (Fujino and Gongyo 2014) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[FujinoGongyo14](#ref-FujinoGongyo14)]. In the algebraic category, the conjecture is proved by Bakker–Filipazzi–Mauri–Tsimerman (Bakker et al. 2025, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[BFMT25](#ref-BFMT25)]. Their discussion of the analytic category retains projectivity of the morphism; the extension to Kähler morphisms is explicitly distinguished in (Bakker et al. 2025, Remarks 6.31 and 7.5) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[BFMT25](#ref-BFMT25)].

We resolve the compact log-smooth case of this Kähler b-semiampleness question. Both the source and the base may be nonprojective. The boundary may have coefficient one, so the relevant Hodge structure can be mixed. The assertion concerns actual holomorphic line bundles: numerical semipositivity alone would not suffice.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Statement

All spaces and maps are complex analytic unless stated otherwise. A *modification* is a proper bimeromorphic holomorphic map. Write $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)_{\mathbb Q}=\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Q$, using additive notation. Equality in this group means an isomorphism of holomorphic line bundles after a common positive integral multiple.

<a id="eq:adjoint"></a>
